SUSPENSION OF TUBERCULOSIS (IRELAND) ORDER OF
1914
Letter was rend from the Department of Agriculture dated
24th August, 1915, No. 2985-15, enc10iing copy of an Order
marlo 'by them suspending as from 1st of Septomlnr, 1915,
until if is otherwise ordered, the operation of the Tuberculoeis
(Ireland) Order of 1914, except Article 12 (1) (diseased animals
not to be sent for shipment). The letter was initialled by tho
Chairman. The following is the Order referred to :—
(435.)
Order of tho Department of Agriculture and Technical
Instruction for Ireland.
(Dated 19th August, 1915).
The Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction for
Ireland, by virtue and in exercise of the powers vested in them
under tho Agriculture and Technical Instruction (Ireland) Act,
1899, the Diseases of Animals Acts, 1894, to 1914, and of every
other power enabling them in this behalf, do order, and it is
hereby ordered, as follows :—
From the first clay of September, nineteen hundred and fif-
teen, and until it is otherwise ordered by the Department, the
operation of tho Order described in the Schedule hereto shall bo
suspended to the extent mentioned in the third column of sueh
Schedule: Provided that such suspension shall not—
(i) affect the previous operation of such Order or anything
duly done or suffered under such Order ; or
(ii) affect any right, privileges, obligation or liability ac-
quired, accrued, or incurred under such Order; or
(iii) affect any penalty, forfeiture or pminighrnent, incurred
in respect of any offence committed against such Order ; or
(iv) affect any investigation, legal proceeding, or remedy
in r«spect of any such right, privilege, obligation, liability,
penalty, forfeiture, or punishment as aforesaid ;
and any such legal proceeding ,or remedy may be
instituted, continued, or enforced, and any such ponalt,y, for-
15
feituro, or punishment may be imposed, as if this Order had not
been made.
In witness whereof tho Department of Agriculture
and Teehnieal Instruction for Ireland have hereunto
set their Official Seal this nineteenth day of August„
nineteen 'hundred and fifteen.
H. G. Smith,
On behalf of the Sooretary.

MAINTENANCE OF LIVE STOCK (IRELAND) ORDER OF 1915.
The Secretary submitted Order of t:he Department of Agriculture
entitled ' 'Maintenance of Live Stock (Ireland) Ordor of 1915,"
dated 24th August, 1915, setting out that for the purpaso of main-
taining a sufficient stock of animals any animal which is visibly
or obviously in-calf or in-pig shall not be slaughterod nor moved
out of Ireland; provided that tho Order shall not. apply to the
slaughter of an animal' under the provisions of tho DiseaN*i of
Animals Acts or the slaughter of an animal desirable on account
of injury €0 tho animal or its illness, or the slaughter of an
animal when considered desirable by tho Dopartanent. The
"Slaughter of Animals (Ireland) Order of 1915" is revoked, and
the Maintenance- of Live Stock Order eomes into operation
on 1st September, 1915.
The Order was initialled by tho
SHEEP REGULATIONS.—DEFAULTERS.
Loiter was read Sergeant Callan, Dunleor, enclosing list
of persons who exposed sheep for sale Dunleer fair on 1st
September; and lotters were from Constable Friel giving
names of persons who exposed sheep for sale at market
on 4th September, without being accompanied by Decluration
that the sheep were dippt«i twice within the Autum Dipping
Tioriod in accordance with tho Dipping (Ireland) Order of'

ing to the sum of C17 ,878 only C6,531 had been collected to
date, and that one of the collectors, out of had only

and it was pointed •out that in September, 1913, when the Com-
'Ilittee granted a similar application, Chat the Iuoc<l.l Govern-
ment Board had refused to entertain it, and the collectors had
to romain out of their fees until the following year.

closing of the collection, and the Secretary was directed to ad-
vise each of the applicants accordingly.

Order.

mouses.

DROGHEDA COURTHOUSE.
The following letter was read and initialled by the (lituirman

I)r€yghoda, 15th Oetober, 1915.
The Secretary,

Dunda 1k.
Ijear Sir,
My Finance (hmmitfoe have now considered your leffer, in
it is suggested' t.haf t.he Corporation should in fill ure pro-
vide fuel, light and water for the portions of Drogheda Courthouse
used by them and their officers.
The suggest ion comes upon t hem with surprise, and they can
only think fliere must be some misunderstanding to account for it.
Perhaps t Ile Counfy Couneil are not fully aware of the circum-
stances under which the Corporation came to use the Courfhoues
for municipal purposes.
Formerly Che "Tholsel" premises were availed of by the Grand
Jury, with the permission of the Corporation, for the purposes
[he present Ctjurfhouse is used for until the erection o! a new
Cou rthonse was decided upon. The Corporation thereupon agreed
with the Grand Jury to lease for ever af a nominal rent of Is.
portion of their principal Market and some of (he A'larket build-
ings, upon the understanding that they would be at liberty to use
the Cou rfhouse for municipal put poses, and thereby be in a
The Courthouse was erected, the
posi€ion to lease the "Tholsel."
Market huildings being altered so as fo form portion of if, and
tlje Corporation moved in. The Grand Jury provided i.he entire
eosf of maintenance, ineluding fuel, light. and wafer. On Che
passing of the T,oea.l Government Act such maintenance, of course.

Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction for

50 and 51 Upper Merriop Street,
Ihiblin, 16th October, 1915.
so. 3670/15.
Sir,
Having regard to the suspension of thc Tuberculosis (Ireland)
79
Order as from the 1st ultimo, the Department find it necessary
to advert t') the question of bhe rate of remuneration attached
tc your office of Clerk to the Local Authority under the
Diseases of Animals Acts, and to direct attention to the terms
Of their letter of 16th December, 1913, in which approval Of the
present rate of remuneration was conveyed.
It, will be observed that ttne Department's approval of the
proposal to attach a salary of CIO() per annu,m to the post was
given subject to revision in the event of a cessation of work
under the Tuberculosis Order. It became necessary, therefore,
-to consider the question of such revision now that the opera tion
of the Order has been suspended for the time being, and that
bivere is, apparently, no reported case of bovine tuberculosis
remaining to be dealt -with in the county.
I have, accordingly, to request that, at' the first opportunity,
you will be so good as to place the matter before your Local
Authority with a view • to their considering what rate of
remuneration should be attached to the office in question in
the altered circumstances at present existing.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
The Secretary,
Louth County Council,
Court House, Dundalk.
The original letter from the Department, dated 18th Decem-
bet', 1913, No. 6147 /13, in which the Department approved of
the appointment of the Secretary to the County Council as
Clerk to the Local Authority under the Diseases of Animals
Acts, was read, in which they expre"ed approval of the
proposal to attach a salary of CIOO per annum to t'he office,
but stipulated that this approval might be subject to revision
in the event of cessation of work under the Tuberculosis (Ire-
land) Order.
The Committee, after considering the proportion of work
under the Tuberculosis Order, which was a very small propor-
Lion of the entire work under the Diseases of Animals Acts,
unanimously decided to reduce the salary of the Clerk to the
Local Authority by the sum of CIO per anntunn, and directed the
Secretary to inform the Department of Sgricul€ure of their de-
eision.
VETERINARY INSPECTORS REPORTS.
The Reports of the Veterinary Inspectors as follows, for toe
months of September and October, were read and initialled

Mr Clinton's Report for September; 1915.
On 1st Septeniber 41 Sheep, the property of Joseph Murphy,
of Derrybeg, were detained at Greenore Port -for scab. The
necessary steps were taken, the affected sheep being slaugh-
tered and the remainder dipped at Greenore and returned to
tlhe owner's farm by order of the Department of Agriculture.
On the same date 48 sheep, the property of Mr P Rooney,
Mountmills, were also detained- at Greenore for scab. The
affected sheep were slaughtered. 'Ille remainder were dipped
at Greenore and returned to Mr Rooney's faun.
Your obedient servant,
J. T. CLINTON,
75
Mr Clinton's Report for October, 1915.
On October 7th 24 sheep {vere detained at Dundalk Port
owing to the presence of scab amongst them. They were the
property of Thomas Malone; l)numghambo, and were consigned
to England. All of them were slaughtered on the premises
by •order of the owner. On Oct'iber 30th 4 lambs suffering from
scab were found in a consignment sent iby Mr J Shevlin to
Dundalk Port from Carrick-macros.s. The necesary steps a re
being taken in the case.
Your olx•dient servant,
J. T. CLINTON.

During the month of September 1 to do•al with one
outbreak cf sheep scab (tihe animals coming from Co. Down).

RATE COLLECTION.
The following letter from the Local Government Poard, in
to the requesf of the Committee for snne€ion to payment,
of
poundage fees to Collectors Hughes, Cluskey, Peter
80
M 'Donnell •and Woods, was read, and payment was made
aecorcling!y : —
Local Government Board, Dublin,
22nd October, 1915.
No. 56,052/1915.
Louth County.
Sir,
The Local Government Board for Ireland have had before
thern your lotter of the 18th instant and previous correspond-
enee with the Lou WI County Council, "relative to the failure of
Collectors Hughes, Cluskey, Peter M 'Donnell and Woods to
lodge tho full amount of their warrants for the past half year
on the prescribed date.
With reference thereto, I am to state that, having regard
io the previous records of these Collectors and their failure to
fulfil tho guarantees given 'by them, the Board feel eonsiderable
hesitation in assenting to their retention in office.
Inasmuch as they lodged within one week of the Board's
lotter of the (6th instant the full amount of t"he half year's
rates, the Board will not now take the extreme step of
removing them. The Board are, however, only prepared IO
allow them a share of their remuneration calculated on the
proportion of the rates actually lodged by them up to tihe 30th
September €0 the t,otal amount of the half-yearly warrant.
The Local Government Board wish the County Council
clearly to understand that this decision is to 'be accepted as
final, and if the Collectors are not prepared to abide by it they
may resign their positions. The Board' cannot admit tlhat
there is any reason why the Collectors of Rates in Louth
81
County should be placed upon a different footing from that of
the Ck)llectors every other County in Ireland.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient servant,
J. E, DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
The SecreUary:
Louth County Council.
OBSTRUCTION AT LURGANGREES ON I"A.NE RIVER.
A report ',vas read frotn the County Surveyor on inquiries
by him, as (lircctecl 'by the Finance Conjmittee, on a
eomplaint Iliade by the deputation from t'he Blackrock fisher-
The County Surveyor reported that there was a lyre.ach
in the em bauklnent along the Fane river south of Mr Murphy's
lauds at Haggardsiown. The embankment forins part of the
Fane Drainage Works, and the 'breach was caused by the illigh
tide of 1st January last. The fishermen state that the drain
110t'th of and adjacent to the toe of the embanktnent is being
ancl
filled up with silt and scraws dug off the enlbankment
thrown into the drain to grow. 'P.te object in closing the drain
is alleged •to be to prevent the 'beaching of boats along the
b auks, and advantage is taken of the breach in the embank-
to dra.in the land water which fornrerly went through
the drain. The tide now rises through the •breach and covers
the adjacent lands. The County Surveyor further stated that
he did not think the County Council could expend money in
repairing the embankment, which is part of the drainage
Works.
The report was initialled by the Clhairman.

MAINTENANCE OF LIVE STOCK (IRELAND) ORDER OF
1915 (No. 2).
'I'he following Order was read and initialled by the Chair-
man :
The Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction
for Ireland by virtue and in exereise, of the powers vested ill
thenl under the Maintenance Of Live Stock Act, 1915, and
the purpose of maintaining a sufficient stock of animals, (IO
hereby order as follows
Sucking Calves 110t [o be Slaughtered or Exported.
1. From and after the (late of commencement of this Order'
no sucking calf shall
5
(a) 'be slaughtered, or be caused or permitted by its owner
to be slaughtered ; or
(b) be moved out of Ireland, or be ea,usecl or permitted by
its owner to -be moved out of Ireland.
Exemptions from Restrictions.
2. The restrictions imposed by the foregoing Article shall not
apply to
(a) .slaugllfer of an anilnal under tho powers conferred by
the Diseases of Anililals Acts, 1894 to 1914, or any Order
made thereunder; or
(b) slaughter of a ma le ealf of any of the following dairy
breocls, that is €0 say, Channel Island,
Ayrshire or Kerry
breeds; or
(e) slaughter of an animal necessary or desira blo on aeeount
Of accidental injury to the anilnal or its illness ; or
(d) slaughter 01 movement out of Ireland or an animal if in
the opinion of the Departntellt of Agriculture and Technical
Instruefion for Ireland the slaughter or movement out of Ire-
land is desirable for any exceptional reason or purpose and
the slaughter or movement is authorised by a license granted
by the Department or by an officer of the Department.
Application of Order.
a. This Order applies to lt•elancl.
C0'nmencement.
4• This Order shall come into operation on the twenty-second
day Of November, nineteen hundred and fifteen.
6
Short Title.
5. This Order may be cited as the ' 'Maintenance of Live
(Ireland) Orcler of 1915 (No. 2).
In witness whereof the Department of Agriculture and
Technical Instruction for Ireland have hereunto set
their Official Seal this seventeentli day of November,
nineteen hundred and fifteen.
T. P. GILL, Secretary.

SHEEP DIPPING PROSECUTION.
The following letter was read and initialled by the Chair-
man
Francis Street, Dundalk,
17th December, 1915.
Dear Sir.
I beg to report that I attended on behalf of the Council at
Dunleer Petty Sessions on Tuesday last, the 14th inst, when
the adjourned ease of the Council v. James O'Hagan, Omoath,
came on for hearing. The defendant was summoned for ex-
posing 81 Sheep for sale at Dunleer fair on the 1st September,
1915, unaccompanied by the necessary Declaration that the
Sheep had 'been dipped within the Autumn Dipping Period.
The magistrates convicted and fined the defendant the sum
of and CI costs.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
Secretary Louth County Council.

My client is a Victualler in this town, and his business takes

farm in County Meath. is owing €0 his continued absecyce
from home that he quite overlooked taking out a licence. Ile
had no intention Of evading tlle law, and has since taken cut
the required licentse. He asks me to express regret for the
oversight and Co state Chat it will not occur again.
In the circumstances I would ask your Council to exercise
their power and reduce the fine to a nominal sum.
Yours faithfully,
FRAS. P. JOHNSTON.
Secretary, Louth County Council.

SHEEP DIPPING (APPOINTMENT OF INSPECTORS)
(IRELAND) ORDER, 1915.
Letter was read from the Department of Agriculture dated
1st December, 4303/15, enclosing copy of Order as above made
24th November, 1915,
$2
The k)llowing are the important provisions of the Order, and
after discussion, it was decided to refer the consideration Of
the Order to the County Council :—
Local Authorities to appoint Inspectors of Sheep Dipping.
Every Local Authority shall temporarily appoint, for service
during Che prescribed dipping periods, as many additional
as may be necessary to enable proper supervision to
be maintained on behalf Of Che Local Authority, with a view
to securing the effective clipping of sheep in their district under
the principal Order.
Department's approval required for appointments.
Appointments under this Order and the remuneration Of
persons appointed shall in all instances be subject to approval
by the Department.
Provisions against delay, etc, in appointing.
If it appears to the Department that a Local Authority has
failed.
(a) to make, within a reasonable time, the appointments
required by this Order, or
(b) foo make a sufficient number of such appointments,
the Department may issue to such Local Authority a Certifi-
cafe,' under the Seal of the Department, specifying what num-
brr of appointments, or of further appointments, as the case
may be, are requisite for the purposes of the Order in the
district of the Local A lithority, and specifying also, if the
Department see fit, the rates of remuneration of the Inspectors
to be appointed and the areas for which they should respectively
act, and the Local Authority, on receipt of such Certificate'
shall proceed, without avoidable delay, to make Che requisite
appointments in accordance with the terms of the Certificate.

C A Duffy, on the irreparable loss sustainoQl by him in the

with the expression of sympathetic references to the deceased
lady.
The Chairman, in putting the resolution, stated that he de
sii•e€l to be specia Ily associated with the sentiments conveyed
therein.
The following resolution was then passed in silence, all the
members standing

Seconded by Patrick Donegan, Esq, J P, and
Resolved—That we learn with great regret of the lamented
death of MIS Duffy, the wife of our esteemed Vice-Chairman,
Mr Charles A Duffy.
As a member of one of the most impor-
tant boards included in the administrative County of Louth,
the deceased Indy had oeeupio«l a, deservedly high position for
her great capacity ; in every good work for the alleviation of
the distressed and the uplifting of the lowly she was stren-
nous worker, while in all things National her patriotislll was
pure ancl nnsullied. We deplore hor loss, and we beg fo fender
to her Ivusbancl and relatives our sincere condolence in their sad
bereavement,
SHEEP DIPPING (APPOINTMENT OF INSPECTORS)
ORDER, 1915.
The Secretary infortned the Council that in accordance with
instructions from the Executive Conunittee, he had written to
the Department that the above Order would be considered ac
the Council meeting this day, and the Department had written
in reply to say that one of their Veterinary Inspectors, Mr Nor-
ris, had been deputed to attend the meeting to explain the Or-
and answer queries. The Chairman then introduced Mr.
orris, who explained the provisions of the Order, which provi-
ded that every local authority should temporarily appoint for
Service during the prescribed dipping periods as many additional
Inspectors as may be necessary to enable proper supervision to
be maintained on behalf of the local autllority with a view
the effective dipping of sheep in their district, and in the event
Of appointments, the Department power under the Order
to make what appointments miglit be considered necessary.
A long discussion ensued in which Messrs Goodman, N T
Was ultimately decided to refer the consideration of the Order
to a "special meeting of the Executive Committee to 'be con-
Vened, so that their report could be considered at the adjourned
meeting of the Council to be held on 24th February.
The Chairman thanked Mr Norris for his attendance and in-
formation, and Mr Norris suitably responded.
ANCIENT MONUMENTS IN COUNTY LOUTH.
Y permission of the Council, deputa [ion from the Arehaeo-
13
logical Society, composed of Messrs G O'Reilly, Drogheda, and
10
H G Tempest, Dundalk, was heard. The members of the depu-
tation informed the Council that various archaeological remains
in Drogheda, notably John's Gate Tower, known as the Butter
Gate, were in a very dilapidated condition, and while the Cor-
porati011 of Drogheda would be pleased to undertake their care'
that under the Acts of Parliament passed for the preservation
of ancient monuments, it was the County Council in whom
their control should be vested. They requested the Council to
undertake charge of these monuments Of antiquity.
After discussion, in which it was pointed out by Mr Wynne
that there were other antiquarian remains in Dundalk and other
places that, required care, the deputation were requested to pre-
pare a list of the various buildings, etc, which they req•uired
the County Conneil €0 undertake control of, when the matter
, would bo considered by the Finance Committee to report to
the Council.
The deputation undertook to furnish this list, and thanked
the Council for the courtesy extended to them. The Chairman
informed them that the Cotmeil would sympathetically consider
tho matter, and do what was possible to preserve those venera-
ble links with the past.

Tho next matter considered by the Council was tho propOS(
agreement between the County Council and the Insurance Com-
mi [tee for the treatment of tuberculosis in County Louth.
draft from of :igrcemenf had been considered by the
Io..is Cotillilitfee and tho Insurance Cornutittee, and a basis

and circulated among the members of the Council,

•4 long discussion ensued in which exception was faken to the

Mr Skeffington also stated that the rate struck of one penny
in the C should only have been half of this amount if the Trea-

Resolved—That we hereby approve of the terms of Agreement
between the. Louth County Council and the County Louth In-
•snranee Committee for the treatment of insured and . exempt
Persons suffering from tuberculosis in the County of Louth, 'by
Which the County Council, in consideration of the payment of
Is '2d per insured person by the Insurance Committee, and the
residue from Administration expenses of the additional ld cre-
dited to them, undertake to provide the services of the Tuber-
ctlJosis Medical Officer (subject to the Agreement), nurses, me-
dieines, shelters, dispensaries, provision in a sanatorium of six
beds for treatment of insured and exempt persons, and in the
Louth County Infirmary of one bed • for insured ancl exempt
SUrgieal cases, and any other treatment for which the balance
Of the funds available may be used, subject to a working
Agreement being made with medical practitioners as domi-
ciliary treatrnent. The term of the Agreentcnt to be twelve
months from 1st January, 1916, to 31st Decernbér, 1916.

the County Schenle for the treatment of tuberculosis to be coni-
pleted, the Secretary was directed to write to the L G 13 to in-
crease the grant for the , erection of the Drogheda Dispensary,
as the amount suggested by the L G considered
entirely inadequate.

RING-NET FISHING IN DUNDALK BAY.
Letter was read from Department of Agriculture dated 24th
January, 265/16 F. 1}. , referring to the resolution passed by [he
Council with reference to above, and requesting particulars of the
damage alleged to have been caused to the local fishing industry
by the method of fishing complained of.

Mr Clinton's Report.
Sir.
During the month of January an outbreak of Scab occurred
in sheep the property of J M 'Nally, Carrickmac•ross, at
Port.
The anitnals were slaughtered by order of the
0 lvner, and the hides disinfected. Restrictions were rernoved
from sheep detained at Heynestown on grazing land the
property of P Hanlon, Piedmont.
With reference to your circular regarding Sheep Dipping
I aun of the opinion that the only efficient Inethod is
by the sn•im bath. So far as I know there are only •l of those
in my district, viz, the one owned by Mr O Keenan at Agha-
1 neon, and those owned by your CounciL at Stonetrough,
n naverna and Carlingford. The last two a.re of course move-
able baths.
A few years ago the Department ordered all the
sheep in Che area between Dundalk and ()meath to be clipped
veterinary supervision in swim "baths. This order was
carried out thoroughly,
with the result that for a couple of
years afterwards not a single ease of Seab was reported in the
Under the present system* there is prae€iea Ily no
district.
supervision over the dipping, and in most cases it is only a
matter of form. Dipping by means of the swim bath is, I
believe, less dangerous €0 the sheep than that done by the
hand bath. In the latter ease the sheep have €0 be turned on
their backs and held forcibly down. Only a fow hundred enn be
done in this way by three men in a day, whereas with the swim
bath two men can easily do over a thonsand a day.
J. T. CLINTON.

Scbolarsbip Committee.
Minutes of Proceedings of the Scholarship Committee at their
Meeting held in the Council Chamber, Courthouse, Dundalk, on
Monday, 21st of February, 1916, af 12 0'clock noon.
Present the Chair: Charles A Duffy, Esq, J P.
Other Members—J T Dolan, M A, and James Connolly, J P,
Esquires.
The Minutes of the previous Moeting of the Committee were
taken as read and signed by the Chui rman.
The Meeting was summoned to consider the conditions of the
Louth County Council Scholarship Scheme for the year 1916-17,
and, after careful consideration, the following Scheme was unani-
m Ously approved of and recommended for adoption by the County
Council
COUNTY COUNCIL SCHOLARSHIP SCHEME, 1916-17.
The Louth County Council offer two Scholarships of a year
each, tenable at the Dublin or other College of the National
University for the Session October, 1916, to June, 1917, and renew-
able for the two succeeding years, and for a fourth year if the
Student follows a Course in Agriculture, or in Industrial or
C Otnmercial Science. The - Scholarships will be open for Com-
2
petition to male and female Students who fulfil the following
requirements :—
(1). Whose parents or guardians are and have been resident in
Co. Louth for the past three years.
(2). Who satisfy the County Council that they are unable Of
their own means to obtain a University Education.
(3). Who have been educated in Ireland.
(4). Who are of good health and character and under 19 years
of age.
(5). Who pass in Irish and reach a standard of honours in the
aggregate of subjects in the Test Examination which gives
proof of abilify enough to put a University Education to
good use.
The Test Examination will be the Intermediate Examination Of
1916, the order of merit being determined by the printed markS
obtained by the applicants in Senior Grade Honours as published
in the Official Results List—a Pass in Irish being essential.
The Scholarships shall be awarded to the two candidates best
qualified in the judgment of the County Council ' on consideration
of merit in this test examination, and of of
other requirements. If either or both of the highest eligible
competitors will not accept Scholarships they shall be awarded
to those next in merit, if qualified.
One or both Scholarships may be altogether withheld if sufficient
merit or need of assistance is not shown.
The Student accepting a Scholarship must pass the Matrictlla•
lion of the National University (unless the University accept
Intermediate Examination as qualification) in 1916, and attend
3
regularly the first year's course in some faculty of the University,
and reside in a hostel or, by special permission of the County
COUncil, in any other residence approved by Clie College authorities.
The College fees Shull be paid direct by the County Council to
the College authorities and the hostel proprietors, the balance, if
any, to be expended on books and other expenses.
The County Council shall obtain from the College authorities
an interim report of the progress and general conduct of each
Student up to the close of the Christmas term, and should the
rePOrt be unsatisfactory as to attendance at lectures or from any
Other cause, the County Council may, on eonsideratiou of the
report, cancel the Scholarship awarded.
In Order to retain a Scholarship for the second and third year
Student must pass the College Examination of that year at. a
Standard of merit to be fixed by the Council in consultation with
the College authorities, and must also pregent a satisfactory report
from the College as to attendance at lectures and general conduct
during thre year.
The County Council shall have complete discretion in applying
Or modifying these rules and in withholding or reducing
Scholarships.
Candidates for Sc'holarships must apply to the Secretary of [lye
County Council before the 15th September, 1916, and the Council
shall meet to consider applications and award Scholarships at the
end of September, 1916.
The Secretary was directed to write flie College authorities for a
report on the Louth County Council Students for the informatiou
of the County Council.

MALICIOUS INJURY CLAIMS.
Letter was read from the Down County Council stating that
at a recent, meeting the matter of very heavy claims for com-'
pensation for malicious iniury amounting to more than Cll 000
which were awarded against the County in cases where the
property had been duly insured against fire was considered ;
that their Council had resolved to approach the Members Of
Parliament for the county with a view to having the law affect-
ing Ireland in this matter amended and brought into line with
the practice prevailing in Great Britain, and requesting the
other County Councils in Ireland to co-operate with this view•
A discussion took place on this letter, and Mr O'Connell sta-
ted that in England where property was insured that no claim
for malicious injury could be maintained.
The following resolution was then passed, Mr Joseph Dolan
dissenting : —
Proposed by N T Mlltvhy, Esq, J P;
Seconded by W T Skeffington, Esq, J P, and
Resolved—That we hereby request the Members of Parlia-
ment for the County of Louth C.o endeavour to have the law re
lating to malicious injuries in Ireland amended and 'brought
into line with the practice prevailing inGreat Britain.
13

ground where if is 7 to 17 feet wide in continuing the rebuilding
of his fence for a further length 52 lineal yards to the south.
As the road is wide at this place (30 to 40 feet between the
fences), T see no objection to his taking in some of the waste
ground providing that he rang€"s the fence in line with the exist-
ing fenee on the south end. This would still leave a grass mar-
gin 9 feet: wide between the south end of the fence a,nd the
watertable.
Tn conneefion with this matter, Wm Kelly, who occupies the
labourer's cottage at the south eud of Mr O'Hare's field, claims
the site of the ruins of the old cottage with the small garden
attached thereto, while Mr ()'llare' asserts fltat this place has
surrendered to him.

qqjttage and garden as a freehold, the fifle to the place appears
to be in dispute.
In order f.o avoid any question of trespass, Mr O'Hare should
give an undertaking not to extend the new fence so far as to
enclose the ruins of the old cottage or the garden.

SIIEEP DIPPING (I.OCAL REGULATIONS) (IRELAND)
ORDER OF 1910.
The Secretary read circular letter from the Department of
Agriculture, dated 25th Febmary, 1916, No. 142—16 V. B., en-
e losing copies of the above Order made by the Department in
ennneetion with sheep dipping. The Olfler gives power to the
Authority to make such regulations as they think fit for
requiring any dipping or double dipping of sheep prescribed
in the principal Order shall be carried out under such conditions
26
and at such times and places as may be specified in a Notice
signed by an InspeeÅor of the Local Authority and served by post
or otherwise on the owner or person in charge of the sheep re-
ferred fo in the Notice.
That this Notice dispenses with the usual Notice of Intention
to Dip.
That each copy of the Regulations made must be approved by
the Department of Agriculture.
ed
That the power €0 make the Regulations shall only be exercis
by the Local Authority or their Connnittee.
That any Inspector of the Local Authority may, subject to their
direction, enter any premises within their district and examine
any sheep thereon, and that such powers of entry and examina-
tion shall be in addition to any other like powers vested in the
Inspector by Che Act of 1894.
That anything done or omitted fo be done in contravention Of
any Regulation made by the Local Authority under this Orden
the owner and [he person in charge of any sheep in respect Of
which the same is done or omitted fo be done, and the lessee
and the occupier of the place in which such sheep are located'
shall be deemed guilty of an offence against the Diseases Of
Animals Act, 1894.
The derms of the Order were discussed by the Committee, and
attention was directed to the first article in the Order—that the
Notice to the owners for dipping appointments should be signed
by an Inspector of the Local Authority; and it was pointed out
that as the Dipping Superintendents would be the; Inspectors
der the principal Order, and that the clerical work might be
27
€'f)hfusing and troublesome to the working superintendents, the
Departmenf suggested that to relieve this difheul€y the Clerk to
the Local Authority might be appointed an Inspector for the
purposes of notification.
After discussion, on the motion of Mr Wm Woods, J P, soccmded
by Mr B L Coleman, J P, it-was unanimously agreed that the
Clerk to the Local Authority be appointed an Inspector under
{he Appointment of Inspectors Order.
NO further arrangements were made about the drafting gf any
Other local regulations, and the Committee adjourned.

The following Memorial was read :
' 'We, the undersigned, request you to have the existing hot
water circulating system in the IReeord Court, Dundalk, U pui
into working ordet at once, before the ensuing March Sessi01bS'

Crown Court, was heated and the Record Court was not heated'

must, re quest bhe matter to receive immediate attention.

the ground level the heat from the pipes was very great, While

in other parts of the Court (luring the recent cold wea
there was extreme coldness. He stated that the heating
tem was very m;tiquated, and that an up-to-date installation o

ager of the Electric Works for the cost of installing electric

lighting, and Chis would be C4ö, and idle probable cost of the

THE SHEEP DIPPING (LOCAL REGULATIONS) (IRELAND)
ORDER OF 1916.
The oonsideration of the above Order of the Department of
Agriculture came up ou special notice on the agenda. The
Secretary read the circular letter from the Department and
51
general terms of the Order. In Clause 1 of the Order it enacted
that the Local Authority may make such regulations as they think
fit for the following purpose, [hat is to say—for requiring that
any dipping or double dipping of sheep prescribed in the principal
Order shall be carried out under such conditions, and at such
time and place as may be specified in a notice signed by an In-
SPOCtor of the Local Anthority and served by post or otherwise
on the owner or person in charge of the sheep referred to in the
notice.
A discussion took place on this Clause, and Mr M'Gée directed
the attention of the Committee [o the confusion that, would
likely arise if this notification were sent to sheep owners for in-
SPeetions at unsuitable times fo [liem. 110 held that, Che proper
Course to adopt would be for the owner fo serve notice, of his
intentiön to dip and Chat the sel«åion of the clafo of dipping
be left, to him, and that the notieo should be sent to the
Local Inspector and not €0 the police as provided in tho principal
Order. This view was held by the Committee, and the following
resolution was passed unanimously
Proposed by James T M'Gee, Esq, J P;
Seconded by Bernard L Coleman, Esq, J P, and
the selection of the date of the dipping be leff
to the Owner of the sheep to be dipped, and that the notification
Of the date be sent to the Local Inspector appointed by the County
under the Sheep Dipping (Appointment of Inspectors)
(Ireland) Order of 1915 and not to the police."

DEATH OF MR EDWARD GOODMAN.
At this stage of the proceedings Che Chairman stated he
wished to propose a, vote of condolence with the relatives Of
Mr Edward Goodman, who was for many years a member of
Council. He referred in most sympathetic terms to the
deceased gentleman's work on the Council, and to the wide
circle of his acquaintances and his interest in his native
county.
He proposed the following resolution
That we learn with regret of the lamented death of
Edward Goodman, who was for many years a respected member
5
Of the Council. His great interest in its work, and in every.
thing that concerned the welfare of our county, has merited
our respect and approbation. We beg to tender to the members
Of his family our sincere condolence in their sad bereavement,
and, after the urgent Statutory business is disposed of, that
We adjourn this meeting as a mark of respect to his memory.
Mr Michael Meado seconded, ancl spoke in feeling terms of
his long and intimate connection with the deceased, anci
lauded his many good qualities.
Tho following members of the Council ancl the Secretary
desired be specially associated with the resolution
Patrick Donegan, J P ; James Wynne, J L P,
mnegan.
Tho resolution then passed in silence,
Standing as n mark of respect.

Louth County.
Sir,

The Local Government Board for Ireland have had
consideration au entry in the Minutes of Proceedings of
the Tnbereulosis Committee of the Louth County Council on

12
clothing to Robert Corr, an uninsured inmate of Peam•ount

ount for the necessary printing under certain heads for the

The following letter was read, and the Secretary was diroc-

County Louth Tuberculosis Committee
of Management,
90, Clanbrassil Street, Dundalk,
4th May, 1916.
oar Sir,—
I am directed by Che Tuberculosis Committee of
anagement to reply to your communication dated the 1st inst,
enclosure copy of letter 17,222-16, dated 20th April, 1916,
from the Local Government Board, relative to certain articles
Of clothing provided for a patient named Carr, at PeamounL
Sanatorium. I am to state that clothing is provided in neces-
s i tous cases by the Authorities of the Institution in which the
treatment is given on the recommendation of the Committee,
Who
are guided in their decision by the Resident Medical Su-
20
perintendent of the Institution reporting that the articles are
necessary for the patient's treatment.
No arrangements have been made with the Sanatorium Au •
thorities to retain any clothing provided for the use of future
patients.
I am to add that generally where clothing is proo-
ded the patient is practically destitute.
Yours faithfully,
V. WARREN,
Secretary, Louth County Council.

LOCAL REGULATIONS.
The following local regulations for the County of Louth
der the Sheep Dipping (Local Regulations) (Ireland) Order Of
1916, were passed unanimously :—
The Department of Agriculture and
Technical Instruction for Ireland.
Sheep Dipping (Local Regulations) (Ireland) Order of 1916.
Administrative County of Louth.
Tn exercise of the powers conferred on them by the ab0Ve-
mentioned Order of the Department of Agriculture and Tech-
nical Instruction for Ireland, and with a view to a better
cution and enforcement of the provisions of the Sheep Dipping
(Ireland) Order of 1915, the County Council of the Administra-
tive Cbunt,y of Louth, being the Local Authority under t'he
Diseases of Animals Acts, have, at a Meeting held the 15th
May, 1916, made the following Regulations, viz
I.—Any owner or person in charge of sheep who is served
post or otherwise with a Notice in the Form in the Schedille
hereto, signed by an Inspector of the Local Authority, shall' in
respect of the sheep referred to in such Notice, carry out' or
cause to be carried out, the dipping or dippings presseribed bY
the Sheep Dipping (I' eland) Order of 1915, under the condi-
iions and at the time and place or times and places specified in
such Notice.
2.—AII dippings taking place under tho foregoing Regula-
tion must be made in the presence of n I,ceal Inspector'
pointed by the LcatlL County ouncii as Local Authority'
29
all sheep must be dipped by thorough immersion as provided in
the principal Order.
Notice of intention to dip served on the Police by a
sheep owner, or person in charge of sheep, will be transmitted
to the Local Inspector appointed by the County Council for the
district where the sheep are located.
It is desirable that sheep owners should arrange with the Lo-
cal Inspectors the days on which they intend to have the dip-
pings done.

The Secretary answered queries of Mr N T Murphy with
reference to the fees paid him for the Motor Licence Duty
collection. He informed the Council that, as the L G B and
Road Board had notified, the Grants made to County Conneils
out Of the Road Board Fund would be based on the amounts
COIIeeted in the various eonnties; that he had increased the
activity in the collection of these duties, and that this factor
had operated in the amount of the Grant made to the Council
for Road the Council received 5 per cent
on the eollection in the county, and the County Council had
Paid over this amount to him and to his predecessor in the
latter's time. He further stated that the amount. collected as
revenue to the County Council for Motor Registration and
riving Licences had been increased from the sum of CGI in
1912 to the sum of in 1915, and that he did not receive
anything whatever on this collection.

DEATH OF MR. LAURENCE M'KEON.
The following resolution was
Proposed by A Duffy, Esq, J P;
Seconded by N T Murfrhy, Esq, and
Resolved—-'l'hat we learn with great regret of the death Of
Mr Laurence M 'Keon, who has been all honoured member Of
the Council for the past fourteen years. His unflinching
advocacy of all National claims, Which during t,he Lond
5
caused him a long term of implåsonment, and his interest in
everything affecting the welfare of our County, has won our
respect and admiration. Although he had reached the patri-
arohal age of 9-5 years, his mental and physical vigour wero
maintained to the very end, and he was looked upon with
affection as the Father of the County Council. We deeply
deplore his loss, and 'beg to tender €0 his family our sincere
SY rnpathy, and, after the urgent business on the Agenda IS
disposed of, that we adjourn this meeting as a mark of respect
to his memory.
Mr M Meade desired to be specially associated the
resolution. which was passed in silence, all members standifig.

PATRICK HUGHES' RATE COLLECTION.
REPORT OF DEPUTATION TO LOCAL GOVERNMENT
BOARD.
The following report was read :—
The deputation appointed by the Finance Committee to
wait on the Local Government Board with reference to the
state of Mr Pat,riek Hughes' rate collection, beg to report. tajat,
accompanied by the Rate Collector's brother, Mr Peter Hughes,
and 'tho representative of the London and Lancashire Firo
Insurance Company, the Sureties of the Collector, were received
by the Local Government Board on Tuesday, the 16th instant.
The facts were laid before the Board as follows
The amount now outstanding is CIIG 7s Od. The irrecoverable
arrears amount to approximately C30, and the amount of
POUndage fees due to Hughes on thc' present and former collee-
tions is C7(). The examination of Mr Hughes' collection books
shows that. there is still due -by various ratepayers a sum ap-
PrOXirnating to $90, which, with Che irrecoverable arrears,
about amount to the balance outstanding.
The Local Government Board refused to sanction the carry-
ing forward to the next collection of Clie irrecoverable arrears,
and stated the Sureties must be called upon to lodge the amount
0 Utstanding; also tihat any arrears of poundages clue the
COIIeetor and the irrecoverable arrears of rates would be paid
direct to the Sureties, according to the terms of the Bond.
The Board -further directed that the Collector be formally
SUSPended by the Council, and when this was done dho Board
Were to be advised, when the matter would be immediately
acted on. Mr Peter Hughes has promised his assistance in the
matter of the lists of irrecoverable arrears and anything
to facilitate bhe County Council and the Sureties.
The following resolution was then passed unanimously
Proposed by Bernard L Colernan, Esq, J P;
Seconded by Bernard Hamill, Esq, J P, and
msoIved—That we hereby suspend .Mr Patrick Hughes fronl
his position at. Rate Collector for collection district No. •5'
Rural District of Dundalk, and that we call upon his Sureties.
the London and Lancasihire Fire Insura nge .Cornpany, €0 lodge
to the credit of the Council the outstanding balance clue 011
foot of his Warrant for the half-year ended 31st March, 1916.

poundage fees due Collectors Cluskey, Woods and Peter
M 'Donnell, in respect, of the collection of poor rate for half-
year ended 30th September, 1915, was read :
Local Government Board, Dublin,
18th May, 1916.
No. 19334/16.
Louth County.
Sir.
'I'he Local Government Board for Ireland bave had before
htem your letter of the 18th ultimo, applying for permission
to pay to Messrs Cluskey, Peter M 'Donnell and John Woods'
Poor Rate Collectors in County Louth, the balance of the
poundage fees -withheld from them in respect of half-year
ended 30th September last.
The Board consider that these Collectors by their failure
fulfil the guarantees which were given by t]hem a short time
ago, have forfeited any title to special consideration.
At tho same time, if these Collectors are now willing to
amends, by showing good progres with their collections during
the current half-year .and by closing punctually, t1110 Board
would be prepared to reconsider the County Council's applica-
tion at a later date. The Collectors nyust, however, clearly
understand that in determining the extent to which the balance
of tho poundage will be refunded to t'hern, strict regard will
be paid to the manner in which they individually carry out
their duties.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient servant,
A. R. BARI,AS,
Secretary ,
geeretary, Louth County Ouncil.

mittee, in May, 1913, in reference fo land on the Newcomen
Estate, Clo@herhöad. There were four years? rates due at the

Executors of the late Fr. Clarke. When the ease chme before
the County Court Judge, Mr Mackay, for the Executors,

NOW T understand dhat all Che money is spent in law costs.
There were two farms sold which realised fourteen hundred
Pounds (El ,400). During the four years the land was derelict,
and Of eourse at the end of each half year I had to lodge the
money as it was part of my warrant.
I dhink in all fairness T should be recouped this money, as I
did all I could in the matter.

Patients appears to the Board undesirable, as it would tend to

available for institutional treatment.
The difficulty, however, might be met by an arrangement
With the Hcuspital Authorities that a sufficient nomber of sets

Oouncil who rna.v require them. -would be prefera;ble that

APPLICATION PROM FAUGHART QUARRY MEN.
The folowing memorial was read
May 27th, 1916.
Sir,
We, the undersigned workmen, employed in the
Louth County Council's quarry at Faughart, wish you to please
Jay before the Council at their next meeting applicati011'
respectfully asking them to grant us an increase of addi-
tional, so as to bring onr wage up to a standard of .CI per wee
Our present weekly wage is I(js, and as you, gentlemen, are
fully aware, the price of food stuffs has gono up by u) per cent
or over, and when ctqleulatod on Chis bae:is our present wage
does not, in purchasing value, exceed IOS per week. Our work
also is both heavy and severe, as is naturally always• the case
in quarry work, and tihe upkeep of wear and tear to our boots'
caused by constant moving on stones, is a very big item Of out-
lay. Besides there is hardly a week in which we do not. lose
time owing to bad weather, and when this happens it leaves
wages still less.
25
We hope, gentlemen, you will give our application favour-
able consideration, and that your decision will be such as to
cause eontentrnent amongst a not overpaid staff of workmen.
We beg to Remain,
Your Servants,
John Halpin, Michael M 'Cann, Patrick Jameson, Jas.
M 'Ardle, Richard O'Hare, Joseph Halpin, Stephen
Fearon, Hugh M'Namee, James Sandes.
To the Secretary Louth County Council.
After discussion the (hmmittee unanimously decided that the
application for an increase could not be acceded to.

No. 1392/16.
Sir.
following letter was read, and initialled by the
Department of Agriculture,
Veterinary Branch,
Dublin, 23rd May, 1916.
Tho last-named tender, being the lowest, was accepted' nn
the following sureties wero approved of :wJulia Breen
William E Hopkins.
havo Io acknowledge thO receipt of your letter of 17th
I n stant asking the Department to give effect €0 a resolution
a doptecl by the Executive Connnittee of your Council in the
following terms : —
32
'"l'hat the owners or persons in charge of Sheep being
exposed for sale, whose names have 'been previously re-
turned to the Council by the Council's Inspector as having
dipped their sheep within ("he prescribed time and in the
proper way, be no longer obliged to aecornpauy same with
any further forms."
In reply I have to state that the point Co which the resolu-
bion is directed has been considered more Chan once previously
by the Department. They have, however, been unable to
discover means of satisfactorily obviating the practical diffi-
the
culties which procedure such as that recommended in
resolution would be bound to involve. If the sheep owner'
when exposing his sheep for sale, were to bring the Dipping
Inspector's certificate with him, the Police, especially if
were not, well known to them, would scarcely be ,satisficql
without some evidence in addition filat the animals exposed
hi n formed part of the lot described in the certificate, and the
need for a declaration supplementary to the certificate would
thus, in any ease, arise. On the other 'nand, if no cloctnnent
were required to be brought with the sheep, and if the police
were to be content merely with verbal statments by owners
or persons in dharge of sheep at fairs that Che animals e.XI
by them had all been clipped as required, Chere can be little
doubt that a door would thereby be opened to widespread
asion of the Order.
In all the circumstances the existing provision requiring
sheep exposed for sale during the nutrket-regulation periods
shall be accompanied by one docurnent only, i.e., a declaration
in specified t,errns relating solely to those animals, is regarded
as that most adapted to the needs of' the case, and the Depart"
ment accordingly regret that they cannot see their way to
accept your Committee's proposal on the subject.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.

directed by the Executive Committee, a special meeting

Dipping Tank was hold in tho Court House, Drogheda,

and Joseph Dolan, Esquire'.

the ken ny Tank, resigning his position. Mr Murphy
directed the attention of the Committee to the fact that owing
36
to the number of private tanks in the disdriet there would be a
large number of inspeetions necessary, and that the entire time
of the person appointed to act as Superintendent and Inspect'Or
would be occupied during the dipping periods, and as
the
Summer dipping period started from the 1st Juno, it would be
absolutely necessary that the appointment should be made at
once.
After discussion, the Committee decided that an advertise-
mcn+o should be inserted in the "Drogheda Argus" on Satur-
day, the 3rd instant, inviting applications for the position, and
recommended that the Inspector and Dipping Superintendent
should be performed by the same person, and that the salary
to be paid should be at the same rate as for the Ardee and
Knockbridge Inspectors, viz, El per week during the d:pping
periods.
Ii; was further recommended, owing to the urgency of the
matter, that appointment should be made at a further
Special Meeting of the Tank Committee, to be held in Drogheda
on Tuesday, the 6th June, at 2 0'clock p m, and that the
Secretary report to the County Council to their Special Meet-
ing to be held on Monday, the 5th June, the findings Of the
Committee for confirmation.
The following resolution was then passed unanimously :
Proposed by Michael Meade, Esq ;
Seconded by James Wynne, Esq, J P, and
Resolved—That we hereby approve of the recommendations
of the Ballymakenny Dipping Tank Local Committee to
Tank

salary, to cover both appointments, at the rate of CI per
37
for tho Summer and Autumn Dipping Periods, and we confirm
the authority given to tho said Local Committee to appoint
the said officer.
FAUGHAR'I' QUARRY.
The County Surveyor informed the Council that the work-
men engaged at Faughart Quarry had ceased work. He stated
that these men had •applied for an increase in their wages,
whiéh at present were 16s per week, and the Finance Commit-
tee had refused to accede to the application, and that, they
bad now struck work.
-'i discussion took place on the matter, but it was considered
that the resolution of the Cbuncil refusing the former appli-
cations of Vhe quarrymen engaged at Jonesboro' and Cocklehill
eonld not be varied now, as all the quarry work was in
COnneetion with the Road Board Grant, and that the rate of
wages to be paid the men was based on the estimate for this
furnished to the Road Board. Eventually it was decided
to make application to the Road Board for an additional grant,
and if t)his request were acceded to, the question of an inerea.se
to the men would be further considered.
Arising out of tho discussion, Mr Finnegan reque.sted in-
f O rmation from the County Surveyor as to whether he had
a Uthorised the overseer of the quarry to question labourers
aPPl.ving for work if t/hey had the permission of their former
0 rnployers to apply for work under the Council.
County Surveyor replied that he merely wished Chat any
Of the Workmen applying for work under the Council had given
uctiee to their former employers of their intention to leave
employment, as complaints had been made to him on
tho subject by road contractors.

DUTIES OF DIPPING ISSPFßYl'ORS LOCAL
REGULATIONS.
Mr B L Coleman stated that there appeared to be somo mis-
apprehension in regard to tho duties of the Inspectors under
the Local Regulationos passed by that Council. He understOOd'
and he believed the Inspectors did also, that they were only fo
inspect dippings carried out at proper swim-baths; but it
peared now that they had to attend at all private dippings
57
ther performed in a swim-bath or in a hand-bath. He did not.
think it would be possible for an Inspector to get through his
Work unless dippings in baths other than swim-baths were done
away with, and Chat those people who had not swim-baths were
compelled to bring their sheep to the county tank.
Mr' P Woods agreed with Mr Coleman's views.
The Secretary informed the Committee that the Inspector at
Ardee Tank had called at the office that morning, and had
handed him six notices of Intention to Dip served on him for
diPPings at places far removed from each other. They were all
to take place on that.day (Monday), and it would be impcxsiible
for 'him to inspect them all. This Inspector also complained
that in a few eases when he had göne to inspect, dippings on the
days specified in the notices, no dippings took place, nor was
•there any preparation made at the place for the clippings. "Ilhe
Seccetary further in formed Che (huncil that he had written
the Department requesting to know whether they approved of
the diPPings in hand-baths, and whether it would be within the
PO wers of the Council to compei owners not having a proper
S Wim-bath to dip at the Council's tank. The following reply to
this lotter was read :—
Dopartrnent of Agriculture,
Veterinary Branch, Dublin,
20th June, 1916.
No. 1810-16.
Sir,
have to acknowledge the receipt of your letter of
19th
instant, in reference to the question of securing in tho pro-
cess of sheep dipping the ' 'thorough immersion" Which the
Sheep Dipping Order requires.
58
In reply I have to state that while dipping in swim baths is'
without doubt, the more expeditious and economical met'hod Of
dipping, it is not impracticable, the Department are advised, by
proper manipulation to effect a thorough immersion in a less
capacious bath. The process in this case is of course a tediOUS
one, and the practice of using small vessels such as you des-
cribe would naturally increase the difficulties of supervision ; but
it does not appear to the Department that it would be compe-
tent for the Local Authority for or other reasons to re-
quire dipping in their district to be carried out in swim baths•
If, however, swim-baths were provided by the Local Authority
it would apparently be open to them, under the Regulations at
present in force in County Louth, to require any owner who had
not suitable means of dipping of his own, to carry out, dipping
in one of these baths. The procedure in such cases would be to
insert the requisite condition as to the manner of dipping in the
Notice specifying tho time and place at which the dipping is re-
quired to be done.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Sæcretary.
Secretary, Louth County Council.
to
After discussion, the Secretary was directed to write again
the Department pointing out the difficulties which had arisen ill
connection with the inspections, and the impossibility of carry-
ing out the work properly unless dipping in hand-baths was
abolished, and requesting a direction in the matter from the
Department. He was also directed to call a meeting Of the
Executive Committee when the reply came to hand.

AUTUMN DOUBLE DIPPING.
following letter was read, and initialled by the
Chairman
so 1475-16.
Dopa rtment of Agriculture,
Veterinary Branch,
Dublin, 30th May, 1916.
Sir,
I have to advert to your letter of 25th instant, and to
60
the resolution which, as you indicate, was unanimously passed
•by your County Council at their adjourned Quarterly Meeting
on that day, in the following terms:—
"Resolved—That as all sheep in this county must be dip-
ped in the presence of a local inspector appointed by
the County Council, we hereby request the Department
to forego that part of their regulations compelling
sheep owners to dip their sheep twice within a certain
time during the, Autumn period, as sheep owners find
this regulation very injurious to heavy breeding
In reply I have to state that the circumstances of the life his-
tory of the insect which produces Sheep Scab render it most
desirable that tho prescribed double dipping at an interval of
not less than seven or more than fourteen days in the Autumn
Period should be persevered in, if the end aimed at, viz—the
eradication of the disease from the country, is, in due course, €0
be attained. The Department could not, therefore, but regard
a suspension of the double-dipping requirement as a retrogradk*
step in existing circumstances, and it is accordingly regretted
that they are unable to the proposal in your Council's
resolution.
At the same time they wish to say that thore is no reason to
consider that the second dipping should necessarily be injurioUS
to '{heavy breeding ewes," especially as it is competent to start
tho arrangements for t'he two dipping-s in such cases as early as
may wished after 31st July.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
Secretary, Louth County Council.

large number of workmen employed at quarries and on road

e Xpeuditure for postage and other petty disbursements.

RATES—NEWCOMEN ESTATE.
With reference to the Rates due Collector Patrick
M 'Cullough out of the above estate, Tn Chancery, which the
Finance Committee, at their meeting held on 29th May, re-
commended to bé refunded to the Collector, subject to the
sanction of the L.G.B., the Secretary ihforrned the Committee
that he had Written the Board Q for their approval, and had
received in replyU1 letter requesting to be informed as to the
nature of the proceedings taken for the recovery out of the
estate of the amount of Rates due, namely, .C13 12s 3d. He
had replied by forwarding copy of lotter received f rom Mr
Daniel O'Connell, who had been acting in the matter, in which
Mr O'Connell stated that he had brought a Civil Bill at
Dundalk Quarter Sessions on .4th June, 1913, claiming Poor
Rates due by the Executors of the late Rev John Clarke, P P'
on the lands of Collystown. Mr Mackay, who defended'
pointed out to Judge Green that the estate was ill Chancer.Y'
and until allocation the Rates could not be paid. His Honor
said .he felt bound to nil the Civil Bill in the circumstanceS•
Subsequently Mr O'Connell had made enquiry at the Office Of
the Chief Clerk in Chancery, and found that until the final
stage is reached, and the costs of the several parties to the
suits are provided for, this account for Rateø cannot dealt
77
with. Proceedings in Chancery are still pending, and will not
be completed before next term.
On receipt of this reply the L.G.B. had written on 30th
June, 1916, No. 31 ,541/16, that under the circumstances
mentioned in Mr O'Connell's letter the Board do not consuer
the Rates irrecoverable, but to meet the wishes of the Council
they assent to the amounts in question being refunded to the
Collector and carried forward for subsequent collection.
The (hmmittee approved of this/ course, and recommended
the refund to the Collector of the amount in question.

SHEEP DIPPING (LOCAL RÉGULATIONS)
As directed by the Committee, the Seéretary had Written COJ
the Department informing them of the difficulties of inspection,
by the Geal Inspectors, of private dippings, which, the
Department had stated, might bé legally performed ih hand
baths.
A very large number of shoep owners adopted tlriS'
method, with the result that on several occasions a numbe+ of
notices of intention to dip, on • the same day and at the same
hour, brit at widely divergent parts of their districts, were
82
served on the Local Inspectors. It was impossible, under
circumstances, for the Inspectors to attend at all the dippingS'
and unless' the dipping in hand baths was abolished it would
be Impossible to carry out the regulations.
The following reply had been received from the Department—
Department of Agriculture,
Voberina,ry Branch,
29th June,
No. -1893/16.
Sir,
1916.
In reply to your further letter of 27th instant respecting
certain difficulties {Rising in oonnection with the supervisi011
of sheep-dipping in County Louth, I have to point out that
the object of the Regulation requiring sheep owners' to carry
out dipping at such time and place, and under such conditions
as the Local Authority may direct, is to place the Local
Authority in a position to overcome the difficulties mentioned'
Such Regulation being in force in County Louth, the Local
Authority may, by the due service of notices thereunder '
arrange the dipping of overy owner's sheep so as to admit Of
th dipping being supervised in each instance by one Of the
Dipping Inspectors. Moreover, in cases where there is reason
to believe that the facilitic»s for dipping on the premises of any
owner are not such as to allow of adequate personal super-
the
vision of the dipping, the Local Authority may require
dipping to be carried out under conditions which admit o
such supervision, e.g., in the public dipping tank in charge Of
the Dipping Inspector.
I have to add that it should be practicable, through
83
Police, to ascertain beforehand Che kind of dipping facilities
which each owner possesses.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
Secretary Louth County Council.
The Secretary was directed to write again to the Department
stating that the Inspectors would be unable to carry out their
duties' under the Local Regulations if dipping in hand baths
was not abolished.

JINSPECTION OF SHEEP DIPPING STATIONS.
Letter was read from the Department of Agriculture dated
7th July, 1916, No. 2004/16, enclosing copy of report of their
Inspector, Mr Norris, M.R.C.V.S., on his inspection Of the
Council's Sheep Dipping Tanks in Carlingford and Omeath
.districts. The Report stated that Mr Norris had visited the.
tank at North Commons, Carlingford, where it is conveniently
Situated at the foot of the Carlingford mountain. The bath
is Of the portable swim-bath typo and has a capacity for 240
gallons of wash. The "run" from the draining pen would be
improved by the addition Of a few battens placed crosswise at
the exit of drainer to give the sheep a foothold as they run out.
There is a drop of about foot from the end of drainer to
ground, and this might be improved so that the "run" would
slope gradually from the end of the drainer. The surface of
the catch pen and the ground surrounding would be improved
by being concreted or sheeted in wood. Tn wet weather the
ground becomes boggy, and in addition to its other drawbacks
this condition would help to foul the bath considerably.
M 'Kevitt, the Inspector in charge, appears to be a satisfactory
Mr Norris also visited Corrakit (()meath) tank, where
the hurdle»s to form the draining pen had not yet arrived. The
Inspector also awaited a supply of Forms, etc., from the County
Council. The Dipping season is later at, Omeath than at,
Carlingford. The location of the tank appears to be eon-
V enient, and the Inspector, Owen Treanor, is an active young
man engaged in sheep farming, who will, no doubt, a
satisfae€ory man for the work.
Mr Norlis, in his general remarks, states that the work of
SUPervision of private dippings will be almost impossible •unless
the Local Authority put in force the provisions of the Local
a fairly large size built of concrete. These would give a better
uncil's tanks, as a, number of owners prefer to dip at home
In mqxst unsuitable tubs.
Mr Norris. considers that the County Council would do well
to consid€•• the question of erecting •permanent swim-bath' of
86
a fairly large size 7miIt of concrete. These fould give a better
service than teniporary baths, and would in the end 'be less
expensive.
The report was initialled by the Chairman.
The Secretary informed the Committee that immediately
after the confirmation of the appointment of Treanor by the
Executive Committee, he had been given all books necessary '
and that the hurdles had been sent to Omeath.

QUARRY AT TULLYALLEN.
The follmlfing letter from Mr B R T Balfour's Solicitor was
read, and referred to the Co. SUrveyor for his report :—
36, South Frederick Street,
Dublin, 19th July, 1916.
Blayney R. T. Balfoui•'s Estate.
Dear Sir,
J have heard from Mr Balfour with reference
to the pass through his land in King William's Gleri from the
Proposed quarry in Mr Roche's land at Tullyallen.
principal objection to giving the permission is that he conr
•Riders that .the beauty of the Glen, which is resorted to by a
large number of tourists, would be greatly spoiled by the heavy
ear ting on the road, which is a narrow one, and that there
Would be
a great deal of obstruction by lorries coming to and
from the quarry.
Also if the pass is fenced the grazing of the lower part of the
Glen will be useless, and if not fenced cattle will 'be able to
stray out on the road, which would be most objectionable.
Mr Balfour thinks that if any other quarry in the neighbour-
hood
Would answer the purpose it wotild be a thousand pities to
do anything that would dpmage one of the beauty spots of the
county.
Se
Yours truly,
E. MA UNSELL.

HALL AT COLLON.
The following report was read and initialled by the Chair-
man : —
County Surveyor's Office,
Dundalk, 22nd July, 1916.
Application by A.O.H. for Site for Hall in Market
Square, Collon.
To the Chairnåan and Gentlemen Finance Committee Meeting'
24th July, 1916.
Gentlemen,
I met the representatives of the applicants
for site in Collon on 11th instant, intending when I had seen
the place to ask the local County Councillors to meet me there•
The County Council does not even maintain the Market square
at Collon. I do not see, therefore, how the Council could be
a position to give title for a building site, apart from other
siderations. I did not consider it necessary then to put the
cal County Councillors to the trouble of meeting me
Your obedient Servant,
THOMAS WALSH.

ASSISTANT SURVEYORS' APPLICATIONS.
As per special notice on the agenda, the following •applica-
tions from Messrs J.mes Dimngoole and J M C Lyons, were
('Onsidered by the Committee, together with report from Co.
Surveyör on the applicmtions as under :—
Dunleer, 22nd July, 1916.
To the Chairman and Members Louth Co. Council.
Gentlemen,
1 respectfully beg to apply for an increase in my
as Assistant County Surveyor, owing to increased du-
ties.
Since the commencement •of your Road Board Scheme (July
1915), the inspection of work in the quarries, seeing to the dis-
tribution of stone along stretches of the Nort'il Road, and the
checking of paysheets for Finance Meetings, considerably ad-
ded to my usual duties. Also the unusual number of neglected
contracts (mostly in remote parts of the district) taken up by
the County Surveyor, . necessarily doubled the number of my
Inspections.
I am now 35 years in the service of your County, and as this
is my second application inside that fairly long period, I hope
for a favourable copsideration, under existing eircumstances.
Your obedient Servant,
JAMES DROMGOOLE.
18
Castle Road, Dundalk,
July 24th, 1916.
•Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
I respectfully beg to apply for an increase
my salary. Twelve years have elapsed since last you had this
matter under consider'ation, and my work has largely increase
in the meantime.
Trusting you will kindly give the matter favourable consi-
deration.
I am, Gentlemen,
Your obedient Servant,
JOHN M C LYONS, B E,
Assißtant Co. Surveyor.
To Louth County Council.
County Survey(fr's Office,
Court House, Dundalk,
5th August, 1916.
To t,he Chairman and Gentlemen,
Finance Committee, 7th August, 1916.
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
In response to your request for a report
from County SVIrve.yor on applications by 'Assistant Surveyors'
I beg to repor€ as follows
So far as I can ascertain there has never been an exact defi-
nition of the duties of Assistant Surveyors by the County
Cotincil. Within the past two years there has Joen undoUb-
tedly a large number of eontraets on whioh Contractors have
failed to carry out their contracts, more especially in M"'
19
Lyons' district, but latterly also in Mr Dromgoole's district.
These neglected contracts undoubtedly impose more work on
the Assistants as on everybody else both in the County Sur-
Veyor's and Seceetary's Departments. A reference to the
-minutes of the County Council proceedings will show the num-
ber of defaulters in each district. Since the outbreak of the
War, owing to labour difficulties and the uncertainties of the
Situation. Contract01? are chary of tendering, and a number of
maintenance and special works have been carried out directly.
In one county I am a percentage of Che expenditure
on such works is given to the Surveyors to compensate them
for extra duties in carrying out the works. -I do not recom-
mend any such course to be introduced into this county.
I would refer to the increases in mileage of roads in contin-
nous maintenance in recent years. - The mileage in each Rural
District in 1898 and 1916 is shown below—to the nearest fur-
long
Ardee R D, 1898 198 miles; 1916—206} miles.
Drogheda R D, 1898—1251 miles; 1916—1381 miles.
Dundalk R D, 1898—2701 1916—304k miles.
It will be seen that the mieage in each Rural District has np-
Preeiably increased since the passing of the Local Government
Aef. The added miles are mostly "fourth-class" roads. In
most other counties such roads are repaired only once in four
or five years, and left to deteriorate again between repairs. As
regards the Road Board Work now in progress, when prepar-
ing the Scheme in 1914, immediately after taking up duty
ere, the Assistants accompanied me when inaking trial bor-
mgs, looking up quarries, etc, and put their local knowledge
at my disposal. The supervision of the work now in progress
20
has of course entailed extra duties on both, but so far more in
Mr Dromgoole's case than in that of Mr Lyons'.
There is no doubt but the work of these officers has increas-
sed since the last revision of their salaries. I recommend their
applications for favourable consideration, and at the same time
I recommend that a list of duties of the office be compiled and
placed on record.
Your obedient Servant,
THOMAS WALSH,
County Surveyor.
The mater was discussed at length by the Committee, and
the various duties of the Assistant Surveyors were gone into in
detail, and approved. The following resolution was then
passed unanimously :—
Proposed by Bernard Hamill, •Esq, J P;

HAY RESTRICTIONS.
Mr B Hamill stated that number of people who had brought
Old crop hay into the market were prevented. by "the police
from disposing of same, on the grounds that the War Supply
Authorities had first claim on it: •He believed that t.he claim
did not apply the old crop, and he suggested that a strong
resolution should be. forwarded to the authorities on the sub-
jeet.
Mr T O'Rourke supported this view, and stated that the per-
son in charge of the War Supplies locally, could not be found
that morning to arrange matters, and considerable inconven-
ienee was caused to both buyers and sellers in consequence.
Eyentually on the motion of Mr Bernard Hamill, J p,
seconded by Mr T O'Rourke, the following resolution was
Passed unanimously, and the Secretary was directed to forward
same to the proper quarter :—
' 'That owing to the very great inconvenience and hardship
caused to the fanners and the public generally by the restric-
placed upon the sale of hay by the Military Authorities,
We request said Authorities to withdraw these resrictions
the ease of old crop hay, and to limit any restrictions made in
regard to the new crop, so that the buyers and sellers may
have a free market for the quantity not required for military
Purposes "

BALLAST FROM CLOGHERIIEAD PIER. •
The following Ieter from Mr D J Rath was read, and the
Secretary was directed to procuie maps from the Board
Works and the Land Commission, showing the 'boundary Of
the Clogherhead Pier and Mr Rath's property,
when
a direction will be given to the Harbour Master on the sub-
ject : —
Clogher Head, 3rd August, 1916.
The Secretary, Louth Co. Council.
Dear Sir,
The Harbour Master at Clogher Head has been
claiming rights on behalf of bhe County Council for the supply
of ballast for ships leaving the harbour. This ballast is taken
from land which I maintain is on my property, and while
have no objection to give every facility for the ballasting Of
vessels by permitting the masters to use the material taken
from this land, I would wish the Council to instruct the Har-
bour Master that any ballast required must be taken with
permission.
Yours faithfully,
DENIS J. RATEL.

INSPECTORS' REPORTS.
The following reports were read
MR. CLINTON'S REPORT.
On July 8th an outbreak of scab occurred in a flock Of 15
sheep, the property of Thomas Thornton, Newtowndarver. The
sheep have been dipped three times, and the necessary mea-
kores were taken to stamp out the disease. An outbreak Of scab
was also detected in a flock of 23 sheep at Dundalk Port,
were consigned €0 England by Mr M Curran, Sligo. The
fected animals were slaughtered and in-contact ones dipped • A
case of swine fever has occurred on the premises of Michael
25
Hanlon, Ramparts, Dundalk. 'l'he matter is being at present
dealt with by the Depattment of Agriculture.
J. T. CLINTON.
MR. DONNELLY'S REPORT.
During the month of July I had two outbreaks of sheep scab
at the Drogheda Port, the anirnals in each case coming from
co. Meath. Tho diseased animals weie slaughtered and the
in-contacts dealt with, and your Secretary was furnished wibh
full reports and particulars in the matter. 'lhere was no ouner
case of contagious disease in animals occurring in this district
during the month.
ANDREW A. DONNELLY.
MR. MAXWELL'S REPORT.
I beg to inform you that I attended the usual fairs and mar-
kets held in the Ardee No. 1 District for the month of July. I
have no case of contagious or- infectious disease to report for
this month.
W. E. MAXWELL.

SHEEP DIPPING TANKS.
The following letter was read, 'but no action was decided
the matter
O. 2077-16.
Sir,
Department of Agriculture,
Veterinary Branch,
Dublin, 24th July, 1916.
Sheep Dipping.
With reference fo your letter of 12th instant, con-
26
Caining a further expression of the views of the Diseases Of
Animals Acts Executive Committee of the Louth County Coun-
cil on the procedure needful to make sheep dipping effective
the County, I have to state that it would be a mistake to sup-
pose that the Defartment regard the dipping in hand-baths as
other than a makeshift arrangement, which it would be unde-
sirable to have recourse to when better means can be provided •
The Department are at one with the Committee in srongly fa-
vouring dipping in central baths under supervision as against
the scattered dipping of small flocks in tubs, which renders
adequate inspection impossible.
But t*he Department consider that the readiest way to attain
the desired end of having dipping carried out at central points
is by the full exercise of the Local Au•thority's powers under
Che Sheep Dipping (Local Regulations) (Ireland) Order of 1916'
in pursuance of which the Inspectors can serve notices requir-
ing dipping to be carried out at the place, etc, specified in tbc
notice. It is hoped that this view of the matter will commend
to
itself to the Committee, and that they will be so good as
T. P. GILL

COUN'I'Y SALARY.
The following application was read
County Surveyor's Office,
Court House, Dundalk,
7th Aygust, 1916.
The Chairrnan -and Gentlenien, Louth County council'
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
I beg to make application to you at your
Quarterly Meeting on 17th inst, for an increase in Iny salary'
which at present stands at, C300.
As you are aware, 1 devote whole time to rny duties
County Surveyor. In the other Counties of Ireland, with only
one or two exceptions, I believe the salaries paid to the Sur-
veyors run from to C9ö().
Since taking up office in County Louth, the present
Board Schetne now in progress was put through, and the carry-
Ing out of the work initiated. There is no need to recall the
lengthy negotiations with the Road Board before satisfactory
arrangement was come to, and our continued insistence to be
treated on the same terms in the matter of a grant as other
Irish counties, was acceded to, nor need I remark upon the
difficultiems, in difficult times, organising and stm•ting the
work in quarries, ete„ including the purchase of' machinery—all
a new departure in this county, and theref01%e the more trouble-
some. This work, needless say, has entailed and does entail
uch running about, and not a little ivory and anxious
thought.
I think it Mill be adtnittecl that the work of the County Sur-
ve.vor at the present time is much more strenuous than in past
days when you paid a larger salary.
Tho Council allow me $100 per annum for travelling. I have,
out Of Iny own pocket, provided and maintain a, motor for use
my work. Allowing for depreciation T am expending more
than the allowance made rne, as practically ever since I book up
duty here the cost of everything has been daily increasing, so
that at the present, time my salary with allowance is not worth
within CIOO of what it was when I entered into office.
I have not canvassed any of the Councillors in regard to this
as I have every confidence in the sense of justice of
the Council, and in its desire to aet fairly 'by its officials.
Your obedient Servant,
. THOMAS WALSH,
County Surveyor.

mouslv •
Proposed by Peter B Moore, Esq, J P;

Thomas Walsh, County Surveyor of the Coupty of Louth, from
