HAY RESTRICTIONS.
At last meeting of the Finance Committee the Secretai.v
had been directed to forward resolution requesting the with-
drawal of restrictions on 1915 crop hay, and the limita t.ion Of
restrictions on 1916 hay. 'l'o this resolution a reply dated 8th
August, had been received, in which the Administra,tive Area
Officer stated while desiring to cause the minimum of inconven-
ience, there could not be any alteration in the terms of the Or-
der. The A. A. O. did not think tho Department would re-
quire any old meadow hay, and he had given instructions to the
Purchasing Officer to release old meadow hay on sight. There
is no restriction whatever on 1915 erop hay. With regard to
the new crop, it was necessary, he said, that the War Depart"
ntent, should have first choice. The quantity required for ship-
ment overseas to the forces is a fractional amount of the avail-
able supply. Everything else will be under license'
and provided War Department maximurn prices are not ex-
eeeded there is at the present moment an absolut+l.v free njar-
ket between buyers and sellers for tho hay not. required for
War Department purposes.

RATE COLLECTION.
In accordance with instrucbions of the Finance Comjnit,ioo at
last meeting, the Secretary had written each County Council
Ireland asking them to kindly answer cert,ain queries with re-
gard to the collection of poor rate in each county. The indivi-
dual replies Were placed before the Committee, and •the Secre-
tary submitted the following surnmary of the answers, which
were ordered to be inserted on the minutes :—
Query What poundage rates are paid Che Rate Collectors,
and if any special conditions are attached to the collection ?
Reply fees vary from '2åd to Is in the C. The
average is generally 6d and 7d in the C. In sorne count,ies a
condition operates that, if n certain proportion of the rate is not,
IOdgod by the 31st July and 31st December in each year, there
is a reduction of the amount of poundage fees from {d Io Ild in
the on the amounts lodged after these periods.
Query 2--DO yor Collectors close t,heir collections pronuptly
by the 31st March aud 30th Septenjber each year ?
Reply 2—1n 2ö eounties all t,he Collectors close pronjptl.y on
the 31st March and 30th September.
In Do
wn and Derry all the Colleetors close for the second mo-
lety on the last day of February.
remaining six counties all the (Å)llectons close on tille
Prescribed dates with a few exceptions—the euuses of failure
ng given as illne.ss, or exceptional circumstances, or a few
Out of a large number of Collectors were a few days late.
Querv a—Do your Collectors attend regularly each f01tnght
to ehe8k their books ?
66
Reply 3—1n every county, with four exceptions, all 'the COI-
lectors check regularly every fortnight. In these latter countieS
some of the Collectors do not check regularly in the first
months of the 'half-years. One of the Collectors in County
Mayo who resides on Clare Island, in. Clew Bay, only attends
every six weeks, but he sends his books to be checked evelT'
fortnight.
Query you experience any difficulty in obtaining
tion of the Incal Government Board for payments of
iV'es where the full amount is not lodged within, the statutory
period ?
Reply 4—Great difficulty is experienced in obtaining sanct'i0U•
for payments of fees in any county whore the Collectors do
lodge in the prescribed period. Galway County Council do nob
pay any poundage, fees on sums lodged outside the half-year.

COC-NCTL'S GRANT FOR TECHNICAL INSTRUCTION.
Letter was read from Mr Easthope, Principal and Secretary.
of the LonfIJ Technical Instruction Committee, stating that
[he •work of the Conunittee was seriously å:npeded by he delay
in the receipt of the grants from the Urban ancl Co. Councils :
that no salaries could be naid to the staff for three months,
which need not have been the ease if the local authorities paid
over their contributions for the half year ending 30th Septetn-
ber in May or June previous, so that application could be made
to the Department for furtller funds. Similarly it was desir-
able that the second moiety of the contribution to 31st March
should be paid in November previous.
'I'he Secretary informed the Connnittee that formerly these
grants were paid at the meetings held in April and November
for the period ended 31st March and 30th: •September respecti-
vely, but that on a former application •by Mr Easthope the
Council decided to pay the grant on 1st April and 1st October
and this had been done since.
The Committee unanimously recommended that the moities
of the grant be paid over in advance as request,ed by the Tech-
nical Conjrnittee.

ANNUAL REPORT OF T. M. O.
'Ilie following report was read, and ordered to be inserted
the minutes
October 31st, 1916.
Gentlemen,
I beg to submit the following report
ing the past year 200 patients were treated at the TuberculOSiS
Dispen.saries in the county, viz—Ardee 25, Cklrlingford and
Omeath 31, DHjgheda 18, and Dundalk 126. 12 patients
12
fused treatment against medical advice, 3 left the county'
died, and •14 were discharged as cured or much improved ; 122
are still under treatment at the dispensaries. 33 patients were
treated at Peamount Sanatorium; 17 of whom wore discharge
as much improved, 3 slightly improved, 4 condition stationary'
and 3 worse; 6 still under treatment. The Louth County
witb
firtuary provided treatment for lö of our surgical
very satisfaet•y results, i.e., 5 were discharged cured, 8 very
much inu»oved, and 2 as stationary. The Branch Dispensary
at •Omeath was opened on the June, 1916, and Drogheda
Dispensary on the 12th, August, 1.9116. Our shelters were in
fairly constant use (luring the year. Aeeommodåltion• for stor-
ing shelters when not in use is needed in Dundalk. A hospital
for the treatment of •advaneed cases is required. When n, suit-
able time presents itself this work should be press•ed on, as ad-
V amod eases are n great source of infection, and no scheme, for
the treatment of tuberculosis can be complete until adequate
provision is mado for these cases.
J. S. WQUILLAN.

Vember I dealt with a large number of outbreaks of' sheep
scab which occurred at the Shipping Ports of Dundalk aud
Greenore. In all t'here were over 380 sheep detained, ancl
with the exception of one low of. tens sheep (one of which was
affected) all of the animals came from outside the county.
The disease did not originate in County Louth, but was detec-
ted in the sheep when they were broußht into the Ports for
When the disease (sheep scab) is found to exist it
shipment.
is my duty to dodain the sheep and to serve; i10tice of" deten-
on the owner. I have then@ see the sheep (both affected
20
and in-contact ones) dipped on two occasions within ten days,
and rnake periodic inspections of them in the meantime.
'Blis, especially in the case of sheep detained at Greenore,
Ineans an enormous •amount of work, and is brought about by
diseased sheep being taken into G). Louth, and over whioh I
can have previously no control, and for whose condition I am
not, responsible. I think it is unfair that I should 'be compelled
to deal with animals which have contracted disease in other
counties, and Which are allowed to be rnoved out of the dis-
tricts where the disease originates. I would respectfully ask
your Council to authorise me (if it is within their power to do.
so) to have diseased sheep sent 'back from the Ports to the dis-
trict from which they came. I am one of the very few Veteri-
nary Inspectors in Ireland Who have two big shipping Ports in
their district.
J. T. CLINTON.

Tho Department of Agriculture is willing to un-

from finely powdered rock, and for this purpose the compact
materials of the Louth-Armagh border seem suitable. Tt is
necessary to break the lock into pieces about to }-ineh diame-
[er, not larger than å-ineh at any rate, preparatory to the fine
grinding that v+ould be done in Dublin. 'I'wo tons of each se-
leeted material would be required.
One of the suitable materials is tho rock of tho County Louth

County Council Quarry at Flurrybridgo.
is in the quarry near the National School at Fork-hill,
Armagh.

quarry, delivered on fo tho Groat Northern Railway for
25

jnaterial, i.e., material under inch or so in diameter, lying in

CARLINGFORD HARBOUR CONSTABLE.
In aeeordanee with the instructions of the Finance Committee
at last meeting, the Secretary had advertised for a Harbour
2
Constable for Carlingford in room of Mr John Fearon, deceased,
at a salary of Æ2() per anryuxn, and au annual allowance of C.5 for
lighting the leading and Pier Lights.
For the position only one application was received—that of
Daniel M 'Kevitt, Newry Street, Carlingford. Fatrner Quin,
P P, gave the applicant an exoellent, character, as (lid also Mr
W Woods, J P, the member for Carlingford District.
It was proposed by Wm Woods, Esq, J P ;
Seconded by Peter Woods, Esq, J P, and
Resolved—That we hereby appoint Daniel M 'Kevitt, of Newry
Street, Carlingford, Harbour Constable at Carlingford at, the

The Secretary stated that he understood that at full moon it

cnee to moonlight, and the Committee, through the Unairmall'
instructed M'Kevitt accordingly, and he undertook to carry
out these instructions, and any other duty imposed by the
Council in connection with the position.
INCREASES TO ROAD CONTRACTORS.
The following letter from tho L G B was read, and refeßed
to the Gunty Council':—
Local Government Board,
Dublin, 14th February, 1917.
No. 6667-1917.
Louth County.
Sir,
The Looal Government Board for Ireland have
before them your letters of the 20th ultimo and brd instant, re-
lative to applications made to tho Louth County COuneiI by
road contractors for an increase of 25 per cent on their contract
prices, and requesting the Board to give a direction to the Coun
cil on the matter.
In reply I am to state that, the Local Government Board
have no 1k)wer to authorise the County Council to pay an in-
creased price to contractors. There is no authority vested in
the Board which would enable them, by concurring in such
payments, to relieve the Council of any liability imposed on
thern by statute.
The Auditor would be bound at the audit of the accounts, to
question any such payment, Inade by the Council, and if 'he al-
lowed it any ratepayer might lodge an appeal against his deci-
Sion, or in the alternative, might apply to the Court of King's
As it
Bench for a Writ of Certiorari to quash allowance.
IS evident that it would be to the financial interest of the rate-
Payers to object to any variations of the terms of a contract
which would result in imposing an extra charge upon them,
the Council will recognise that they would incur considerable
risk in granting an increased price to a cont:actor, unless t'hey
Were in a position to clearly justify their action in doing so.
Any existing contract which contains a clause enabling it to
be terminated with the sanction of the Local Government Board
can, of course, be. cancelled witlEt11e sanction of this Depart-
ment, but generally speaking a contract can only be annulled
With the consent of the two contracting parties, and then only
on grounds which will satisfy the Auditor that any additional
charge to the ratepayers by reason of the action of the local au-
thority was not excessive or unreasonable.
It may be that, in the case of particular roads, the work of
maintenance may be dispensed with in Bhe present crisis, and
in such eases the cancellation of the contract might be justified
and the contractor paid for the work performed by him up to
date. If, however, it is found necessavy to proceed with the
maintenance of particular roads, the question of cancelling the
contracts would depend to some ext,ent on the date on which
the contracts wero entered into. If entered into before the war,
the Council might take that fact into consideration, though it
would not, by itself, be a sufficient ground for cancelling a con-
tract.
The view which the Board take in regard to contracts in eX-
isting circumstances, is that where a contract entered into
since tT1e outbreak of war was for a period of two or more years'
it might be reasonable to assume that a contractor could not at
the time of taking the contract hove foreseen that the wax
would extend for so long a period, and, accordingly, an appli-
cation to cancel such a contract at any time after expiration Of
the first year dru ing which it is running might possibly be jus-
tified. In the case of a contract now running for a period Of a
year or less, the Board think that the contractor should have
been in a position to tender at a rate which would secure his
own interest during the eurrency of the contract, and that it
should not be cancelled unless, for exceptional reasons, the
cessity for taking this course could shown.
In the case of a contract now running for two or more years
where the contractor alleges t'hat owing to war conditions he is
of
unable to complete it at the stipulated price, the Board are
opinion that a safe course for the (huncil to adopt might' be to
ascertain from their contractor the minimum price at which he
would be willing to carry on the work for Ulte period of the
tract, and they should then issue advertisements inviting
5
ders from outside persons for this work. If they obtain tenders
at a price less than that specified by the existing contractor,
the Council might then, with 'his consent, put an end to the
contract, and enter into a new one with the accepted tenderer ;
if, on the other hand, all the tenders received were at a price
higher than tihat specified by the existing contractor, a fresh
contract might then be entered into with the contractor at, the
new price, as agreed to by the Council. .13y adopting this ar-
rangement the Council would be in a vo.•y strong position to
iustify to the auditor any increased charge thrown on the rate-
payers, or to defend their action if any allowance made by that
Officer should be brought either before the Local Government
Board or the Tligh Court.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
Secretary, Louth Co. Council.
LABOUR FOR TILLAGE.
following letter was read and referred to the County
Council : —
Local Government Board,
Dublin, 7th February, 1917.
No. 29-M: 17.
Miscellaneous.
Sir,
The Local Government Board for Ireland desire to
state that, as your County Council are no doubt aware, it is of
the utmost importance in the present crisis that every available
6
effort should be made to set freo the maximum amount of la-
bour for tillage purposes.
This is a motter in which direct assistance can be given by
County Councils and County Surveyors, by releasing for agri-
cultural work as many as possible of tho labourers now em-
ployed on road maintenance, or other road schemes. How far
it may be safe to go in this direction in any county is a ques-
lion which to some extent depends on t;he existing condition of
the county roads. Tn many counties the bulk of the road ma-
terial has already been spread, and during the next few months
labour can perhaps be confined to surface work on roads which
have to bear heavy traffic. Steam rolling operations eau in
case usually be 'held over without disadvantage until the sum-
mer months when labour for agricultural work may not be so
urgently required.
The Board feel confident that, tho County Council will recog-
nise the urgency of t'his matter, and that they will not retain
in their employment any labourers who in the present year can
bo more usefully employed on tillage operations.
Suitable arrangements with this Object can no doubt at once
be made if a meeting of your County Council is not, to be held' at
an early date.
I am Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS,
Secretary.
To each County Council.
7
INCREASE TO QUARRY WORKERS.
The Committee had again under consideration the applioa-
tions on behalf of the workers in Braganstown Quarry for. an
increase of wages from 18s to 22s weekly.
The County Surveyor asked permission, which was granted,
to reply to a statement made at last meeting about the hour of
starting work in the quarry, which, it was stated, was nine
o'clock, as the whistle was heard going at that hour. He said
that the workers began at, eight o'clock in winter time. Some
of the men, however, did not turn up till nine, but the lost
time was of course deducted from their wages. Those w'ho diå
turn in at, eight were set to work moving stone, etc, and when
the full gang assembled the whistle blew for them to begin
work at the engine and crusher. This explanation was consi-
dered quite satisfactory •by the Committee.
On the question of the increase the Surveyor stated, in reply
to the Chairman, that he expected to be finished at Bragans-
town quarry in a fortnight, and that it would take over a month
to finish the work on the Lurgangreen road. The men began
work at eight o'clock in tihe quarries, as stated, and worked
till six o'clock, except on Saturday, when they worked till two
o'clock, with twenty minutes off for lunch.
After discussion, the following resolution was passed unani-
mously
Proposed by Bernard Hamill, Esq, J P;
Seconded by William Woods, Esq, J P, and
Resolved —That we recommend €0 the County Council that
tho wages of workers engaged in all the quarries managed by
tho County Council, be increased from 18s to 22s per week, pro-
vided that the men agree to work on Saturday as on the other
8
week days, that is, from 8 0'clock till 6 0'clock; if t,his be not
agreed to, that the increase be to 20s weekly only, the hours of
work to be as at present.
SCREENINGS.
The County Surveyor wrote stating that he had
for screenings and 11 stone from Mr E Caraher, Mayne, and
Mr C P Kearney, of Wottonstown. If he 'had any surplus of
these materials he would charge, 2s Gd and 3s Gd per load res-
pectively, with the Council's permission.
The Committee unanimously agreed that Che Co. Surveyor

no urgency in

DAYLIGHT SAVING BILL.
The following resolution was passed, Mr T V M 'Quillan dis-
senting, and the foih;wing gentlemen were appointed a deputa-
tion to wait on the Secretary of the Department to lay their
views him Joseph Dolan, P M'Gee, J P; J.
M J P; T .J P; W T Skeffington, J P; J T
M 'Gee, J P; M Meade; B Hamill, J P, and the, Chairman and
Secretary
Proposed by James M 'Cnrthy, Esq, J P;
Seconded by S T Murphy, Es•q, J P. a,nd
Resolved— That we, the Connfy Council of Louth, emphati-
cally protest, against the re-enactment (Yi' the Daylight Saving
Bill for Ireland, for thc fo!lowing reasons the hay and corn
harvests farm work cannot be safely commenced before 9 0'clock
a m, dews and nigat frosts rendering the grass and grain damp
and quite unfit for careful harvesting; this fact will apply with
increased force this year, when the MiliLary Authorities ancl
ot,hej' purchasers of farm produce will require it supplied in dry
condition. The weeding of farm crops and [he singling and
hoeing of swedes, mangels and turnips, etc, cannot be done ill
the early morning hours. Owing to t,he dartlP state of Che land
anil leaves of the plants, hands aro most reluctant to undertake
Cho work before 9 c'clock, a m. Tillage farmers are painfully
aware that the steppage of farm work at, 4.30 p m, 0111 Style,
wastes Lhe two best hours of the surmner afternoon for saving
both hay and corn, and too often results in incalculable damage
to crops. Farm hands will not work, unless most reluctantly,
especially in the neighbourhood of towns and villages, after the
hour at which tradesmen and other workers engaged in indus-
trial pursuits cease working, and the result is the .10,ss of
nrost, precious hours daily or roughly a day every week, at a
cost to the community and farmers which it would be hard to
estimate. That the further imposition of Greenwich Time on
Ireland is an additional and serious hindrance to the work Of
ti!lage farmers and loss Co all dependent on tlheir labours, un-
called for, unnecessary, and opposed Io rutional laws; Chat we
resent it, and joc Its pr•mnpt repea.l and that a deputation
from this Council an I the Louth Farming Societies be appointed
to wait, on the Clue! Secretary aud Vice-President of the Irish
Board of Agriculture, .to place their views on the matter before
Chem. 'l'haL copies o/ this resolution be sent to every county
Council in Ireland, ropeetfully and cordially inviting their
nost co-operation anci united support in having a National griev
1b
ance and serious loss to the entire people of Ireland promptly
remedied. That, this resolution be also forwarded to the Prime
Minister, Vice-President of the Irish Board of Agriculture, Mr
John Redmond, Si.• J Lonsdale, and Che Members of Parlia-
ment for the Coue r,ies xuout}h, Meath, and Dublin County and
City.

CINEMATOGRAPH EXHIBITIONS.
Letter was read from the Chief Secretary's Office enclosing
model conditions for insertion in Cinematograph Licences, and
requesting the approval of the Council of same. The conditions
give power to the licensing authority to object and to cause the
withdrawal from exhibitions under their control of objectionable
films. The conditions also cover the control of posters of film
subjects, and the lighting of the interior of picture houses.
The conditions were unanimously recommended for approval
by the (hunty Council.

34
Report on Annagassan Pier and Harbour by the County
Surveyor of I-A)uth.
To the Chairman and Gentlemen, Finance Meeting,
2nd April, 1917.
Mr Clmirman 'Gentlemen,
Following on your instructions asking be
informed as to the state of the Works, Anna.gassan Harbour and
Pier, I beg Io report:—
STRUCTURAL WORKVBREAKWA'I'ER AND PIER.
These have been constructed fairly conformable to plan. Some
paving on part of the quay has not so far been done. In con-
versation with Mr Griffith, the Department's Engineer (acting
for Mr Hassard), last, September, I agreed that this could be
held over for bhe present and attention devoted to the channel.
It is not a large iten•.
A sloping retainmg Wall shown at the western end of the basin
and running nortlyv, ards to the River Dee shown on plan, has
not l»en constracted, and is not necewsary—in fact the Depart-
ment has been reqvested by the Harbour Commissioners to
dredge a cut to thc river through where it was at first' proposed
to erect this wall.
The quay wall is shown on plan extended westwards to the
end of the County Road to form a retaining wall. This has not
been carried to the full length shown on plan. Tt was left ill
its present state in 1.915, When work ceased for that year.
'believe the Department's then Engineer, Mr Oliver, had under
consideration bhe di€(iging of the cut above-mentioned to the
River Dee. This ii out, as project,ed then, would have
necessitated modifications in the structure of Che wall.
Some additions io the structural works have been made, viz :
a short concrete cros wall to act, as a breakwater [o prevent
waves from breaking over the eastern return wall of the quay;
portion of the bre;ykwater is in course of being raised higher
than at first proposed.
With these structural works the County Council are directly
eoneerned, as an agreement in respect of these exists 'between
the Department ard the County Council.
As to the Champl and Basin—I have not as yet made a sur-
vey of the works.
No dredging ha.s Leen done since 1915, as I am given to un-
derstand it was impossible to get necessary repairs to the dred-
ger carried out in time for work last year.
As to the work has Laken place in the
basin dredged. Sinec 1915 probably about four feet of mud has
been deposited. North of the east end of the breakwater near
the entrance to the basin where, the River Dee meets the Ohan-
nel, a considerable deposit of gravel and stones has accumulated
I arn informed that a, vessel drawing nine feet of water re-
eently grounded n .e this point and had to be lightened before
she was able to gec into the basin alongside the quay. rme
depth of water Fl a Nided for in the place is 13 feet at high
water O.S.T.
Tho Depa rtrnent now inform Che Chmmissioners that unless
the dues sanctioned by the Board of Trade are collected, no fur-
ther work will be carried out on bhe Harl»ur. The Commis-
sioners in view of [he siltnge, and more particu laxly of Che de-
posit above-mentioned at the entrance Co the 'basin, 10th to
charge harbour clues, first,ly, for fear of actions for damages to
vessels entering tie harbour as it, stands; secondly, lest apart
from responsibi!ity the harbour may be dammed, and ship ow-
ners later on, even when the harbour is in more finished state,
refuse to send their boats there.
The Commissioners, there-
fore, suggest that they Oharge quay clues only for the present.
Having regard tc the very considerable siltage, T suggest
that the Department be again asked fo consider the dredging
of the cut 30 ft. from the present basin to the river, as
suggested in March, 1916, to their Engineer, Sir John Griffith,
reporting on the works in 1915, was not unfavourable to the
making of this cut.
You:• obedient Servant,
THOMAS WALSH,
County Surveyor.

ASYLUM CAPITATION GRANT.
The following letters were read, and the, Secretary was direc-
ted to write the Treasury and urge the necessity for increasing
the Capitation Gtmntfor lunatics owing to the greatly increased
cost, of maintenance : —
Richmond District Asylum,
Dublin, 23rd March, 1917.
Dear Sir,
I am instructed by the Joint Committee of this
Asylum to transmit you a copy of a letter from the City Trea-
surer of the Dublin Corporation, with reference to the Capita-
tion Grant to Asylums, and I am to express a hope that you uill
take action with a view of representing to Government the
equity of a re-adjusianent to ensure full payment of 4s per head
per week. As you are aware the contri'bution is paid direct
to County Councils from the Local Taxation Account, that this
Aooount has for many years shown a. shrinking revenue with an
expanding liability, and that indications point to further
abatements in the payment to Councils.
Yours truly,
W. J. MURPHY,
Chief Clerk.
The Secretary,
Louth County Council.
(Copy).
City Treasurer's ()ffee,
Municipal Buildings, Dublin.
Dear Sir,
The Treasury Commissioners, T understand, have
recognised the diftc;ulty of maintaining . Ilildren committed bo
Industrial SOhools by conceding an extra Capitation Granf of
fid per head per we provided the loeal authorify also agree
[o increase their capitation grant by Gd.
This is a recognition of the difficulties of the Managers of
these Schools, which applies with even greater foreo to t,he
maintenance of patients in District Asylums.
As you are aware the original capitation grant, through the
insufficiency of tho Local Taxation Account, has dwindled to
about 3s 4d from the 4s fixed so far back, I think, as 1874. I
suggest that you bling the matter before your Board at, the
earliest possible ,moment as they might be disposed to represent;
the facts to tho Government and seek an increased grant.
They might at least mike good Che deficiency for the current
year, and ease t,he estimates of the Asylum for next year by a
similar concession.
Yours faithfully,
EDMUND W. BYRNE,
City Treasurer.
W. J. Murphy, Esq,
Chief Clerk, Richmond District Asylum.

With reference to proposal No. 43, the Co. Surveyor reported
that the Road Board work, owing to increased cost of labour,
materials, ei,c, could not be carried out for the amount original-
Iy estimated. Ardee Rural District Council 'had passed an
additional proposal of Clö() and Dundalk a proposal of El,000
for the work. The Road Board should be asked to give a
further grant owing to the exceptional circumstances.
After discussion, it was unanimously decided to instruct the
Secretary to approach the Road Board in the matter, and to
state that it was absolutely necessary that in order to com-
plete the work satisfactorily, in view of the change in condi-
tions since the original estimate for the work was passed, that
the Road Board should give a further Grant for the work.

TECHNICAL GRANT.
The following letter from the Department was road and or-
dered to be inserted on the Minutes
No. T. 3637/17.
Department of Agriculture, Dublin,
12th APIil,. 1917.
Sir,—
Adverting to your letter of the 27th February last,
regardigg thea suggested revision 'Of the administrative are+h,
5
in which the, County Lou:tih Jointn Technical Instruction. Com-
Inittee and .the Drogheda Technical Instruction Tommittee. at
present conduct schemes Technical Instruction, and to.. the.
Conference which the Assistant Secretary in respect of Tewhni-
eal Instruction had Uitly representatives of the con-
corned on the '28th- ultimo, I have to state that the Depart-
ment have given careful consideration fo the proposal that
the annual contribution of C150 granted by t,he County Louth
County Council to the Joint Scheme Should be divided so that
C7ö should be made available for the Drogheda Technical
Sohools, and also to the proposal that another Joint Commit-
tee be established to administer a scheme for Drogheda Urban
District and the Rural Districts of the South of the County.
The Department would •be unable to approve of tho formation
of a second Joint Committee to' administer a scheme in the
South .of the County.
It is understood that the objectu underlying the proposal is
to secure that more instruction should be given in the rural
The Depart-
areas, especially in the South of the County.
ment feel that any such change could readily be secured with-
out establishing •mother Committee, and they are willing €0
approve of the inclusion in the present Joint Scheme of a
clause securing that classes should be given in j t/he southern
Rural Districts involving an expenditure of not less than $.75
during the academie year. They are, however, of the opinion
that such a division of the funds would not entirely meet the
6
need. The contd'ibution from your Council to the sc!heme has,
since its initiation, been very small, and this circumstance has
necessarily limited the operation of the scheme in the rural
areas. They would suggest, therefore, the allocation of at
least C250 per annum from the funds of your Council for the
purposes of the scheme administered by the County Joint
Technical Instruction Committee.
The Department desire
that the Committee should consider whether a portion of the
funds which it is hoped may be allocated should not be em-
ployed in assisting students from the Rural Districts to attend
classes in tlhe Technical Schools at Dundalk' and Drogheda. It
is understood that, in the case of the former school, there
are eighty students attending from the districts outside the
Urban area, while there are thirty such students attending
the Drogheda Technical School. An increased number of
students might be induced to attend the Schools if a small aid
were given to them in the form of a scholarship.
I have to state that therb is nothing to prevent the services
of any t,eadher engaged by the Drogheda Technical Instruction
Committee being utilised for dhe conduct of classes in the rural
districts where such an arrangement would be advantageous
from the poiqt, of view of efficiency and economy. In such a
case the County Louth Joint Technical Instruction Committee
would arrange with the Drogheda Technical Instruction
mittee for a payment in respect of Ghe services of the teacher
so employed.
I am, Sir,
Your oikBdient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary, Louth (huncil.

COMMITTAL TO INDUSTRIAL SCHOOL.
Letter was read from the Rev Father M 'Keone, Adm, Dun-
dalk, stating that at the Children's Court, Dundalk, he would

SEA WALL AT GREENORE.
In accordance with instructions of the Finance
Che County Surveyor, in company with Mr William Woods,
J P, inspe€ted the damage done by erosion to the sea wall at
Greenore, and reported that as the wall did not adjoin the
8
County Road, and as it was originally 'built by the owner of
the lands it protected, he oould not see how the Council could
be legally liable in the matter, nor did he think t,here was any
Governmental Fund out of which a grant could be procured
for the purpose of repairing the wall.
ne report was initialled by the Chairman.
